10.3
Hybrid Indistinguishability (Proof of
Theorem

19
)

In this section, we prove the indistinguishability of the first and the last hybrid. That is, we prove
Theorem

19
.

First we give the hybrid indistinguishability lemmata.

Lemma 5
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}\equiv\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}
.

Proof.

This is straightforward by the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{1,u(\lambda)+1},\mathcal{A}_{2,u(\lambda)+1}
.
∎

Lemma 6
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{1}\equiv\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,1,0}
.

Proof.

This is straightforward by the definition of
\mathcal{B}_{{1},{0}},\mathcal{B}_{{2},{0}}
.
∎

Lemma 7
.

For all
\ell\in[u(\lambda)+1]
,
\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,0}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,1}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)}
.

We prove this lemma in
Section

11
.

Lemma 8
.

For all
\ell\in[u(\lambda)+1]
,
\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,1}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,2}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)}
.

We prove this lemma in
Section

10.3.1
.

Lemma 9
.

For all
\ell\in[u(\lambda)+1]
,
\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,2}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,3}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)}
.

We prove this lemma in
Section

10.3.2
.

Lemma 10
.

For all
\ell\in[u(\lambda)+1]
,
\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,3}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,4}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)}
.

We prove this lemma in
Section

10.3.3
.

Lemma 11
.

For all
\ell\in[u(\lambda)+1]
,
{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,4}}\equiv{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell+1,0}}
.

Proof.

Observe that, for the internal state,
\mathcal{B}_{{2},{\ell+1}}
produces the exact same mixed state as the one produced by
\mathcal{Q}_{2,\ell}
in
{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,4}}
since it is conditioning on the same output message.
∎

Lemma 12
.

\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,u(\lambda)+2,0}\equiv\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
.

Proof.

By definition.
∎

Finally, combining these lemmas gives us

\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}_{0}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{4\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)}

which proves
Theorem

19
.

10.3.1
Proof of
Lemma

8

In this section, we prove
Lemma

8
. That is, we prove that for all
\ell\in[u(\lambda)+1]
,
\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,1}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,2}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)}
. Throughout the rest of the proof, fix any
\ell\in[u(\lambda)+1]
.

Note that the difference between the hybrids
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,1},\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,2}
is that in
{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,1}}
we run
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell}
with the actual oracle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Sign}}^{*}
whereas in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,2}
, we use
\mathcal{R}_{2,\ell}
which simulates
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell}
with an estimated (by
\mathcal{R}_{\ell,1}
) oracle. We will use the incompressibility property of
\mathsf{OSS}
(
Definition

21
) to show that this only makes a difference of
\frac{1}{\lambda\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)}
statistical difference. In this proof whenever we refer to incompressibility we will be referring to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Sign}}
incompressibility of
\mathsf{OSS}
. We make two notes about this hybrid. First, note that this hybrid is only needed for the LOCC leakage-resilience security: it can be removed in case of non-interactive security. Second, this hybrid indistinguishability can be proven directly using strong
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Sign}}
incompressibility of any
\mathsf{OSS}
(the proof would be essentially the same as
Section

11.1
), but here we use regular incompressibility and the properties of our actual
\mathsf{OSS}
scheme, rather than strong incompressibility.

Let
\gamma_{vk,z}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell}}
be the total query weight of
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell}(Y^{\ell})
to
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Sign}}
in
{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,1}}
on the input
vk,z
.
We also define the following.

\displaystyle\alpha_{vk,z}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell}}=\begin{cases}&\gamma_{vk,z}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell}},\text{ if }\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Sign}}(vk,z)\neq 0\\
&0,\text{ if }\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Sign}}(vk,z)=0\end{cases}

We also define
W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign}}
. Here, let
X_{sign}
denote the values sampled during the execution of
\mathcal{R}_{1,\ell}
, and let
L_{sign}
denote its projection onto its elements’ first components (i.e.
L_{sign}=\{vk:(vk,z)\in X_{sign}\}
). Similarly, for any
vk
, let
S(vk)
denote
\mathsf{Span}(\{v:(vk,v)\in X_{sign}\})\setminus\{0\}
.

\displaystyle W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}vk,z:\\
z\not\in S(vk)\end{subarray}}\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell}}_{vk,z}

We now claim the following.

Claim 1
.

For all sufficiently large
\lambda>0
,

\displaystyle\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign}}[W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign}}]\leq\frac{1}{128\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}}.

Proof.

We will prove this using the incompressibility property of
\mathsf{OSS}
: Consider the following adversaries
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}
for it.
\mathcal{P}_{1}
simulates the hybrid
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,1}
until
\mathcal{C}_{1,\ell}
produces its output
Y^{\ell}
, and then outputs
Y^{\ell}
. Then,
\mathcal{P}_{2}
receives this value and runs
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell}
with it. When considering
\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2}
for incompressibility of
\mathsf{OSS}
, we assume that
\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2}
are only oracle-adversaries for
\mathsf{OSS}
, and the other oracles are just simulated, e.g. they sample
H_{0}
in advance and both use this sample to simulate the oracle outputs of
\mathcal{O}_{0}^{*}
. Observe that both
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}
can perform their simulations with their respective oracles (e.g.
\mathcal{P}_{2}
only queries
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{Sign}}^{*},\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{Ver}}^{*}
and does not query
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{GenQKey}}^{*}
).

Let
\mathfrak{L}
be the incompressibility list for the signing oracle as in
Definition

21
, and we know
|\mathfrak{L}|=s_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)=32768\cdot\lambda^{5}\cdot(\ell\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}\cdot(t(\lambda)\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}
since
\mathcal{P}_{1}
makes at most
s_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)/2
queries to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{GenQKey}}^{*}
. The list depends on the oracle itself and the
leakage

Y^{\ell}
, but we will not write this dependence explicitly for conciseness.

\displaystyle W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign},\mathfrak{L}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}vk,z:\\
z\not\in S(vk)\\
vk\not\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell}}_{vk,z}

\displaystyle W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign},\mathfrak{L}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}vk,z:\\
z\not\in S(vk)\\
vk\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell}}_{vk,z}

Now we claim

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign},\mathfrak{L}}]\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda).

To see this, consider the value

A=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}vk,z:\\
vk\not\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell}}_{vk,z}

Observe that with probability
1
, this is greater than or equal to
W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign},\mathfrak{L}}
. Then, observe that
\mathds{E}[A]\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
by the incompressibility property of
\mathsf{OSS}
.

Now we claim that for all sufficiently large
\lambda>0

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign},\mathfrak{L}}]\leq\frac{1}{256\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}}.

To prove this, we first define for
i\in[s_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)]
,

\displaystyle Q_{i}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign},\mathfrak{L}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}vk,z:\\
z\not\in S(vk)\\
vk=\mathsf{Item}_{i}(\mathfrak{L})\end{subarray}}\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell}}_{vk,z}

We claim
\mathds{E}[Q_{i}]\leq\frac{1}{256\cdot s_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}}
for all
i\in[s_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)]
. Observe that with probability
1
we have
W=W_{1}+\sum_{i\in[s_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)]}Q_{i}
, thus, proving this claim will complete our proof.

Now fix some
i^{*}\in[s_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)]
. Fix any value
{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell}
. We will prove the above for any such fixed values. Note that fixing these also fixes
\mathfrak{L}
and thus
\mathsf{Item}_{i^{*}}(\mathfrak{L})
- denote this as
vk^{*}
. There are
r_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)=\lambda^{8}\cdot\ell^{2}\cdot(t(\lambda))^{4}\cdot(n(\lambda))^{2}\cdot(t(\lambda)\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}
iterations during the run of
\mathcal{R}_{\ell,1}
, and we will consider these iterations in
n(\lambda)+1

groups
: we have
\frac{r_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)}{n(\lambda)+1}
iterations in each group. Now let
\mathsf{GOOD}
denote the event that
Q_{i^{*}}\leq\frac{1}{256\cdot\lambda\cdot s_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}}
. We will prove that
\Pr[\mathsf{GOOD}]\geq 1-2\exp{-\lambda}
. For each
j\in[n+1]
, define the event
E_{j}
to be the event that during the
j
-th group, a new element is added to
X_{sign}
, and define
E^{\prime}_{j}
to be the event that
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}vk,z:\\
z\not\in S_{j}(vk^{*})\end{subarray}}\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell}}_{vk^{*},z}>\frac{1}{256\cdot\lambda\cdot s_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}}
where
S_{j}
denotes
\mathsf{Span}(\{v:(vk,v)\in X_{sign}\})\setminus\{0\}
for the value of
X_{sign}
at the beginning of the
j
-th group. It is easy to see that
\left(\vee_{j=1}^{n+1}\overline{E^{\prime}_{j}}\right)\implies\mathsf{GOOD}
. Similarly, it is easy to see that
E_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge E_{n}\implies\overline{E^{\prime}_{n+1}}
, since the subspace
\mathsf{Colspan}(A_{vk^{*}})^{\perp}
is of dimension at most
n
, so, once
n
linearly independent elements are added, there cannot be any vectors outside
\mathsf{Colspan}(A_{vk^{*}})^{\perp}
. Note that combining these two, we also get
E_{1}\wedge\dots\wedge E_{n}\implies\mathsf{GOOD}
. Finally, we claim that
\Pr[E_{j}|E^{\prime}_{j}\wedge E]\geq 1-\exp{-\lambda\cdot 2n}
for all
j\in[n]
and any event
E
such that
\Pr[E]>0
. For now, we will assume this and complete the proof, and later we will prove it. First, observe that
\Pr[\bigwedge_{j=1}^{n}E_{j}|\bigwedge_{j=1}^{n}E_{j}^{\prime}]\geq(1-\exp{-\lambda\cdot 2n})^{n}\geq 1-\exp{-\lambda}
by above. This also means
\Pr[\mathsf{GOOD}|\bigwedge_{j=1}^{n}E_{j}^{\prime}]\geq 1-\exp{-\lambda}
. Also recall that
\overline{\bigwedge_{j=1}^{n}E_{j}^{\prime}}\implies\mathsf{GOOD}
. Combining these two gives us
\Pr[\mathsf{GOOD}]\geq 1-2\exp{-\lambda}
.

Finally, we prove
\Pr[E_{j}|E^{\prime}_{j}\wedge E]\geq 1-\exp{-\lambda\cdot 2n}
for all
j\in[n]
and any event
E
such that
\Pr[E]>0
. To see this, observe that during the
j
-th group, we measure the query of the adversary for
\frac{r_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)}{n(\lambda)+1}
times. Since the weight on
good elements
(i.e. elements that would be added to our list and thus cause
E_{j}
to occur) is at least
\frac{1}{256\cdot\lambda\cdot s_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}}
when conditioned on
E^{\prime}_{j}
, we get the desired result since
r_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)\geq 1024\cdot(n^{2}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))\cdot\lambda^{2}\cdot s_{OSS,\ell}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}
.
∎

Now we claim that the lemma follows from
Claim

1
. To see this, observe that the two hybrids only differ on the oracles they use for
\mathcal{C}_{\ell,2}
:
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,1}
uses
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Sign}}^{*}
whereas
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,2}
uses the estimated oracle created by
\mathcal{R}_{\ell,1}
. Observe that these oracles can differ only on inputs
vk,z
such that
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Sign}}^{*}(vk,z)\neq 0
(i.e.
z\in\mathsf{Colspan}(A_{vk})^{\perp}\setminus\{0\}
) but
z\not\in S(vk)
. Now observe that
W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell},X_{sign}}
is an upper bound on the total weight on the inputs on which the oracles might differ. Thus, the lemma follows by the Jensen’s inequality, the fact that
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell}
makes at most
t(\lambda)
queries to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Sign}}^{*}
and
Theorem

6
.

10.3.2
Proof of
Lemma

9

In this section, we prove
Lemma

9
. That is, we prove that for all
\ell\in[u(\lambda)+1]
,
\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,2}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,3}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)}
. Throughout the rest of the proof, fix any
\ell\in[u(\lambda)+1]
.

Observe that the difference between the two hybrids is that in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,2}
we run
\mathcal{R}_{2,\ell}
directly, whereas in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,3}
we instead run
\mathcal{Q}_{2,\ell}
which simulates
\mathcal{R}_{2,\ell}
while using an estimated oracle for its queries to
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{3}
. We will use the incompressibility property of
H_{0},H_{1}
to show that this only makes a difference of
\frac{1}{\lambda\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)}
in statistical distance.

Let
\gamma_{ivk,y_{0},y_{1},z}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},G^{\ell}}
denote the total query weight of
\mathcal{R}_{2,\ell}(G^{\ell})
to the oracle
\mathcal{O}_{2}^{*}
on input
ivk,y_{0},y_{1},z
. We also define the following.

\displaystyle\alpha_{ivk,z}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},G^{\ell}}=\sum_{y_{0},y_{1}:\mathcal{O}^{*}_{2}(ivk,y_{0},y_{1},z)\neq\bot}\gamma_{ivk,y_{0},y_{1},z}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},G^{\ell}}

Now let
(X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}})_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell],j^{\prime}\in[t(\lambda)]}
,
(X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{1,j^{\prime}})_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell],j^{\prime}\in[t(\lambda)]}
,
(D^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}})_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}
,
(D^{\ell^{\prime}}_{1,j^{\prime}})_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}
be the estimated lists and databases in
V^{\ell}
(or equivalently,
G^{\ell}
) when it is fully unwrapped and parsed. Let
X_{3},D_{3}
be the estimated list and database inside
V^{\ell}
that is sampled by
\mathcal{Q}_{1,\ell}
. With abuse of notation, sometimes we will refer to the projection of the sets
X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{\cdot,j^{\prime}}
onto their elements’ (which are tuples) subcomponents also as
X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{\cdot,j^{\prime}}
(e.g. we will write
X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{2,j^{\prime}}
to mean the set of pairs
(ivk,z)
obtained by projecting each tuple in the actual set
X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{2,j^{\prime}}
to its first and fourth elements) - however, the meaning will clear from context. Now we claim the following.

Claim 2
.

\Pr\left[\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell],j^{\prime}\in[t(\lambda)]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)\bigcap\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell],j^{\prime}\in[t(\lambda)]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{1,j^{\prime}}\right)=\emptyset\right]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(\lambda).

Proof.

Observe that if this intersection is not empty, then there is
ivk
such that
ivk\in X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}
and
ivk\in X^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}_{1,j^{\prime\prime}}
for some
\ell^{\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime},j^{\prime\prime}
. However, by construction we know that this implies that we can obtain
isig_{0},isig_{1}
from
V^{\ell}
such that
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Ver}^{*}}(ivk,0,isig_{0})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Ver}^{*}}(ivk,1,isig_{1})=1
. Since
V^{\ell}
is obtained by making polynomially many queries to the oracles of
\mathsf{OSS}
, the claim follows by the one-shot security of
\mathsf{OSS}
.
∎

Now we define the following (some dependencies are implicit to keep the notation concise).

\displaystyle W_{0}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},G^{\ell},X_{3}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,z:\\
ivk\not\in\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell],j^{\prime}\in[t(\lambda)]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)\\
(ivk,z)\not\in X_{3}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,z}

\displaystyle W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},G^{\ell},X_{3}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,z:\\
ivk\not\in\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell],j^{\prime}\in[t(\lambda)]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{1,j^{\prime}}\right)\\
(ivk,z)\not\in X_{3}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,z}

\displaystyle W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},G^{\ell},X_{3}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,z:\\
ivk\not\in\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell],j^{\prime}\in[t(\lambda)]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)\bigcap\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell],j^{\prime}\in[t(\lambda)]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{1,j^{\prime}}\right)\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,z}

Now we make the following claims.

Claim 3
.

\mathds{E}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},G^{\ell},X_{3}}]\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda).

(1)

Proof.

This simply follows from the definition of
W_{2}
and
Claim

2
.
∎

Claim 4
.

For all sufficiently large
\lambda>0
.

\displaystyle\mathds{E}[W_{0}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},G^{\ell},X_{3}}]\leq\frac{1}{128\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathds{E}[W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},G^{\ell},X_{3}}]\leq\frac{1}{128\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

Proof.

We will only prove the first one (using incompressibility of the random oracle
H_{0}
), and the second bound follows by the same argument (but this time relying on the incompressibility of
H_{1}
).

Now we will use the incompressibility property (
Theorem

15
) of the random oracle
H_{0}
with the predicate
P
defined as
P(ivk||z)=1
if
ivk\in S
, where
S=\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell],j^{\prime}\in[t(\lambda)]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)
. Note that this set
S
is exactly the set of prefixes on which our adversary has
section queries
(otherwise it only makes normal queries and verification queries). In more detail, let
\mathcal{P}_{1}
be the adversary that simulates
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,2}
until the point where
\mathcal{R}_{1,\ell}
outputs its message, and it also outputs
S
as its predicate. Then
\mathcal{P}_{2}
simulates
\mathcal{R}_{2,\ell}
. We invoke
Theorem

15
for this pair of adversaries, and let
\mathfrak{L}
denote the incompressibility list that is promised to exist, and we know
|\mathfrak{L}|=\ell\cdot t(\lambda)
since
\mathcal{P}_{1}
makes at most
\ell\cdot t(\lambda)
normal queries. We then split
W_{0}
into two summands
U_{0},U_{1}
:

\displaystyle U_{0}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},G^{\ell},X_{3}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,z:\\
ivk\not\in\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell],j^{\prime}\in[t(\lambda)]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)\\
(ivk,z)\not\in X_{3}\\
(ivk,z)\not\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,isig}

\displaystyle U_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},G^{\ell},X_{3}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,z:\\
ivk\not\in\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell],j^{\prime}\in[t(\lambda)]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)\\
(ivk,z)\not\in X_{3}\\
(ivk,z)\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,isig}

By the incompressibility lemma, we get
\mathds{E}[U_{0}]\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
. Now, we will focus on bounding
U_{1}
.

First we claim that, for all
{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},G^{\ell}
, with overwhelming probability we have that any
ivk,z
that satisfies

\alpha_{ivk,z}\geq\frac{1}{512\cdot(t(\lambda)\cdot\ell)\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

is contained in
X_{3}
. To see this, observe that
X_{3}
is created by measuring a random query of
\mathcal{R}_{\ell,2}
to
\mathcal{O}_{3}
for
512\cdot\lambda\cdot(t^{2}(\lambda)\cdot\ell)\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}
times. Thus, for any
ivk,z
such that
\alpha_{ivk,z}
satisfies the above condition, the probability that
(ivk,z)\not\in X_{3}
is at most
\exp{-\lambda}
. Now define
\mathsf{GOOD}
to be the event that all
(ivk,z)\in\mathfrak{L}
that satisfies

\alpha_{ivk,z}\geq\frac{1}{512\cdot(t(\lambda)\cdot\ell)\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

are included in
X_{3}
. Since this is true with probability
1-\exp\{-\lambda\}
for each
ivk,z\in\mathfrak{L}
individually, and since
|\mathfrak{L}|
is polynomial, we have that
\Pr[\mathsf{GOOD}]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
.

Now we claim

\mathds{E}[U_{1}\big|\mathsf{GOOD}]\leq\frac{1}{512\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

To see this, first by linearity of expectation we have

\displaystyle\mathds{E}[U_{1}\big|\mathsf{GOOD}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,z:\\
ivk\not\in\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell],j^{\prime}\in[t(\lambda)]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)\\
(ivk,z)\not\in X_{3}\\
(ivk,z)\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\mathds{E}[\alpha_{ivk,z}|\mathsf{GOOD}]

However, by definition of
\mathsf{GOOD}
, when
\mathsf{GOOD}
occurs, we have

ivk,z\in\mathfrak{L}\setminus X_{3}\implies\alpha_{ivk,z}\leq\frac{1}{512\cdot(t(\lambda)\cdot\ell)\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

Since
|\mathfrak{L}|=\ell\cdot t(\lambda)
, we obtain the desired upper bound on expectation conditioned on
\mathsf{GOOD}
. Since
\Pr[\mathsf{GOOD}]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
, we also get

\mathds{E}[U_{1}]\leq\frac{1}{256\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

as desired.
∎

Observe that
Lemma

16
follows from the above claims. To see this, observe that
W_{0}+W_{1}+W_{2}
is an upper bound on the total weight on which the two different oracles in the hybrids might differ. Thus the result follows by the linearity of expectation,
Theorem

6
and Jensen’s inequality.

10.3.3
Proof of
Lemma

10

In this section, we prove
Lemma

10
. That is, we prove that for all
\ell\in[u(\lambda)+1]
,
\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,3}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,4}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)}
. Throughout the rest of the proof, fix any
\ell\in[u(\lambda)+1]
.

First, note that the difference between the hybrids
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,3},\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,4}
is that in
{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,3}}
we run
\mathcal{Q}_{2,\ell}
with the actual oracle
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{*}
whereas in
{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,4}}
we use the simulated oracle
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
. We will use the incompressibility property of the random oracle
H_{\mathsf{sig}}
(
Theorem

15
) to show that this only makes a difference of
\frac{1}{\lambda\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)}
statistical difference. In this proof whenever we refer to incompressibility we will be referring to that of
H_{\mathsf{sig}}
.

For the incompressibility property, consider the following adversaries
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}
.
\mathcal{P}_{1}
simulates the hybrid
\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,3}
until
\mathcal{Q}_{1,\ell}
produces its output
V^{\ell}
, and then outputs
V^{\ell}
. Then,
\mathcal{P}_{2}
receives this value and runs
\mathcal{Q}_{2,\ell}
with it. When considering
\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2}
for incompressibility of
H_{\mathsf{sig}}
, we assume that
\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2}
are only oracle-adversaries for
H_{\mathsf{sig}}
and the verifier (as in
Theorem

15
) of
H_{\mathsf{sig}}
respectively, and the other oracles are just simulated, e.g. they sample
H_{0}
in advance and both use this sample to simulate the oracle calls to
\mathcal{O}_{0}^{*}
. Observe that both
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}
can perform their simulations with their respective oracles, e.g.,
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{*}
is simply the verification oracle of
H_{\mathsf{sig}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}
does not query
H_{\mathsf{sig}}
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}
be the incompressibility list as in
Theorem

15
, and we know
|\mathfrak{L}|=s_{4,\ell}(\lambda)=512\cdot\lambda\cdot(t^{2}(\lambda)\cdot\ell^{2}(\lambda))\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}
since that many queries to
H_{\mathsf{sig}}
are made by
\mathcal{P}_{1}
. The list depends on the oracle itself and the
leakage

V^{\ell}
, but we will not write this dependence explicitly for conciseness.

Let
\gamma_{m,y}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell}}
be the total query weight of
\mathcal{Q}_{2,\ell}(V^{\ell})
to
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{*}
in
{\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell,2}}
on the input
m,y
. Define
\alpha_{m}
as

\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell}}_{m}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y:\mathcal{O}_{4}^{*}(m,y)=1\end{subarray}}\gamma^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell}}_{m,y}.

Now define
W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4}},W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}_{1},W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}_{2}
as

\displaystyle W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m:\\
m\not\in X^{\ell}_{4}\end{subarray}}\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell}}_{m}

\displaystyle W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m:\\
m\not\in X^{\ell}_{4}\\
m\not\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell}}_{m}

\displaystyle W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m:\\
m\not\in X^{\ell}_{4}\\
m\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell}}_{m}

In the definition above, and in the rest of the proof, by abuse of notation, we write
m\in X^{\ell}_{4}
to denote that there exists a pair
(m,y)\in X^{\ell}_{4}
for some
y
.

We claim the following.

Claim 5
.

For all sufficiently large
\lambda>0
,

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}[W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4}}]\leq\frac{1}{128\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}}.

Observe that once we prove this, the lemma follows by
Theorem

6
and by the Jensen’s inequality since the oracles
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{*}
and
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
in these hybrids can possibly differ only on inputs
(m,y)
such that
(m,y)\not\in X^{\ell}_{4}
and
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{*}(m,y)=1
, and
W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4}}
is an upper bound on the total weight on such inputs and
\mathcal{Q}_{2,\ell}
makes at most
t(\lambda)
queries to the oracle
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{*}
.

To prove
Claim

5
, we make the following two other claims.

Claim 6
.

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}]\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda).

Proof.

Consider the value.

A=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m:\\
m\not\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell}}_{m}

Observe that this value is always greater than or equal to
W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}
.
Also observe that
\mathds{E}[A]\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
by the incompressibility of
H_{\mathsf{sig}}
.
∎

Claim 7
.

For all sufficiently large
\lambda>0
:

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}]\leq\frac{1}{256\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}}.

Proof.

First we claim that, for all
{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell}}
, with overwhelming probability we have that any
m\in\mathcal{M}
that satisfies

\alpha_{m}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell}}\geq\frac{1}{512\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}\cdot s_{4,\ell}(\lambda)}

is contained in
X^{\ell}_{4}
. To see this, observe that
X^{\ell}_{4}
is obtained by measuring a random query of
\mathcal{Q}_{2,\ell}(V^{\ell})
to
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{*}
for
r_{4,\ell}(\lambda)=512^{2}\cdot(t(\lambda))^{6}\cdot\lambda^{8}\cdot(u(\lambda))^{4}\cdot(q^{*}(\lambda))^{4}\cdot(\ell)^{2}
times and adding to the set if the pair
m,y
obtained from the measurement is
valid
(i.e.
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{4}(m,y)=1
). Thus, for any
m\in\mathcal{M}
that satisfies the above condition, the probability that
m\not\in X^{\ell}_{4}
is at most
\exp{-\numestbyind{4}{\ell}\cdot\frac{\alpha_{m}^{\fireora, \lastadvout{\hybindex}}}{\numfourqueries}}
. Since we have

1-\exp{\numestbyind{4}{\ell}\cdot\frac{1}{ \numfourqueries\cdot 512\cdot\numfourqueries\cdot(\lambda\cdot{\numrounds}\cdot\origwinpoly(\lambda))^2\cdot\promisedlistsize{4}{\ell}} }=1-\exp{-\lambda}

this claim follows.

Now define
\mathsf{GOOD}
to be the event that all
m\in\mathfrak{L}
that satisfies

\alpha_{m}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell}}\geq\frac{1}{512\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}\cdot s_{4,\ell}(\lambda)}

are included in
X^{\ell}_{4}
. Since this is true with probability
1-\exp{-\lambda}
for each
m\in\mathfrak{L}
and since
\mathfrak{L}
is of polynomial size, we have that
\Pr[\mathsf{GOOD}]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
.

Now we claim

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}\big|\mathsf{GOOD}]\leq\frac{1}{512\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}}.

To see this, first by linearity of expectation we have

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}\big|\mathsf{GOOD}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m:\\
m\not\in X^{\ell}_{4}\\
m\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\mathds{E}[\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell}}_{m}\big|\mathsf{GOOD}].

However, by definition of
\mathsf{GOOD}
, when
\mathsf{GOOD}
occurs, we have

m\in\mathfrak{L}\setminus X^{\ell}_{4}\implies\alpha^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell}}_{m}\leq\frac{1}{512\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}\cdot s_{4,\ell}(\lambda)}

Since
|\mathfrak{L}|=s_{4,\ell}(\lambda)
, we obtain the desired upper bound on
\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}\big|\mathsf{GOOD}]
. Since
\Pr[\mathsf{GOOD}]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
, we get

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}]\leq\frac{1}{256\cdot t(\lambda)\cdot(\lambda\cdot{u(\lambda)}\cdot q^{*}(\lambda))^{2}}.

∎

Now observe that
Claim

5
follows immediately from
Claim

6
and
Claim

7
since
W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4}}=W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}+W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}
for all
{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},V^{\ell},X^{\ell}_{4},\mathfrak{L}}
(i.e. with probability
1
). This completes the proof of
Claim

5
, and then the lemma follows as discussed above.

10.4
Proof of
Theorem

20

In this section, we prove security in the last hybrid. That is, we prove
Theorem

20
, which says
\Pr[\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}=1]\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
.

First we prove the following lemma.

Theorem 21
.

Let
H:\{0,1\}^{v(\lambda)}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
be modeled as a quantum random oracle,
and let
v(\lambda)
be a superlogarithmic function.

For any adversaries
(\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2})
,
consider the following experiment.

\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}}(\lambda)

1.

Sample a random function
H:\{0,1\}^{v(\lambda)}\rightarrow\{0,1\}^{\lambda}
.

2.

\mathcal{A}_{1}
is given quantum oracle access to
H
and outputs a message register
\mathsf{R}
.

3.

Sample
m^{*}\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{v(\lambda)}
.

4.

\mathcal{A}_{2}
does not have access to
H
, and it receives
(\mathsf{R},m^{*})
and outputs
y^{*}
.

5.

Output
1
if
y^{*}=H(m^{*})
, and
0
otherwise.

Then, for any query-bounded
\mathcal{A}_{1}
,

\Pr\!\left[\mathsf{Exp}_{\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{A}_{2}}(\lambda)=1\right]\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda).

Proof.

Since
m^{*}
is unpredictable to
\mathcal{A}_{1}
, the result follows by
Theorem

7
.
∎

Observe that in
\mathsf{Hyb}_{3}
, to win the game, the adversary needs to output a value
sig^{*}
such that
H_{\mathsf{sig}}(m^{*})=sig^{*}
. Also observe that in this hybrid, the receiver adversary
\mathcal{B}_{{2},{u(\lambda)+1}}
does not have access to any oracles, and the sender adversary
\mathcal{B}_{{1},{u(\lambda)+1}}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}}}
makes polynomially many queries to the random oracle
H_{\mathsf{sig}}
and to
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{*}
(which can be simulated by querying
H_{\mathsf{sig}}
). Thus the result follows by
Theorem

21
.

.

\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{0,2}\equiv\mathsf{Hyb}_{2,\ell^{*},0}

,

.

,
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell,t(\lambda),2}
.

Lemma 15
.

For
i\in[t(\lambda)]
,
\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i-1,2}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i,0}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda)}
and
\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i,0}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i,1}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda)}
.

We prove this lemma in
Section

11.1
.

Lemma 16
.

For
i\in[t(\lambda)]
,
\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i,1}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i,2}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda)}

We prove this lemma in

.

follows by simply combining these lemmata.

11.1
Proof of
Lemma

15

In this section, we prove
Lemma

15
. We will only explicitly prove the first part, and the second part follows similarly.

Fix any
i^{*}\in\{1,\dots,t(\lambda)\}
. Thus, we will prove
\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i^{*}-1,2}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i^{*},0}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda)}
. Observe that the difference between the two hybrids is that in
{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i^{*}-1,2}}
we run
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}
directly, whereas in
{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i^{*},0}}
we instead run
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell^{*},i^{*},0}
which simulates
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}
while using an estimated oracle for its first query to
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{0}
. We will use the strong incompressibility property of
\mathsf{OSS}
to show that this only makes a difference of
\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda)}
in statistical distance. In this proof, whenever we refer to incompressibility, we will be referring to the strong incompressibility of
\mathsf{OSS}
.
For the strong incompressibility property of
\mathsf{OSS}
, consider the following adversaries
\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2}
.
\mathcal{P}_{1}
simulates
\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i^{*}-1,2}
until
Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1}
is produced, and outputs
Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1}
. Then,
\mathcal{P}_{2}
receives this value and runs
\mathcal{C}_{\ell^{*},2,i^{*}-1,2}
with it until right after first query to
\mathcal{O}_{0}^{*}
. When considering
\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2}
for incompressibility of
\mathsf{OSS}
, we assume that
\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2}
are only oracle-adversaries for
\mathsf{OSS}
, and the other oracles are just simulated, e.g. they sample
H_{0},H_{1},H_{\mathsf{sig}}
in advance and both use this sample to simulate the oracle calls to relevant oracles.

Now let
\mathfrak{L}
denote the strong incompressibility list for these adversaries, and we know
|\mathfrak{L}|
=
s_{OSS,\ell^{*}}(\lambda)=2048\cdot\lambda\cdot(t^{2}(\lambda)\cdot\ell^{*})\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}\cdot(\ell^{*}-1)+2\cdot i^{*}
since
\mathcal{P}_{1}
makes at most
\frac{s_{OSS,\ell^{*}}(\lambda)}{2}
calls to
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{GenQKey}}
. The list depends on the oracle itself and the
leakage

Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1}
, but we will
not write this dependence explicitly for conciseness.

Let
\gamma_{ivk,isig,z}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}}
denote the query amplitude of
\mathcal{C}_{\ell^{*},2,i^{*}-1,2}(Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2})
on input
ivk,isig,z
during its first query to
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{0}
. We also define the following. Here,
X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}
is the list sampled during execution of
\mathcal{C}_{1,\ell^{*},i^{*},0}
.

\displaystyle\alpha_{ivk,isig}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}}=\begin{cases}&|\gamma_{ivk,isig}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}}|^{2},\text{ if }\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Ver}}(ivk,0,isig)=1\\
&0,\text{ if }\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Ver}}(ivk,0,isig)=0\end{cases}

\displaystyle W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,isig:\\
(ivk,isig)\not\in X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,isig}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}}

\displaystyle W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,isig:\\
(ivk,isig)\not\in X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}\\
(ivk,isig)\not\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,isig}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}}

\displaystyle W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,isig:\\
(ivk,isig)\not\in X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}\\
(ivk,isig)\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,isig}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}}

Now we make the following claims.

Claim 8
.

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}]\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda).

Proof.

Observe that with probability
1
, we have

\displaystyle W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,isig:\\
(ivk,isig)\not\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,isig}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}}

However, expectation of the value on the right is negligible by incompressibility of
\mathsf{OSS}
(with the adversaries
\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2}
).
∎

Claim 9
.

For all sufficiently large
\lambda>0
.

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}]\leq\frac{1}{256\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

Proof.

First we claim that, for all
{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}
, with overwhelming probability all
ivk,isig
that satisfies

\alpha_{ivk,isig}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}}\geq\frac{1}{512\cdot s_{OSS,\ell^{*}}(\lambda)\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

is contained in
X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}
. To see this, observe that
X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}
is created by measuring the first query of
\mathcal{C}_{\ell^{*},2,i^{*}-1,2}
to
\mathcal{O}_{0}
for
r_{OSS,\ell^{*}}(\lambda)=4194304\cdot\lambda^{10}\cdot t^{2}(\lambda)\cdot(\ell^{*})^{5}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{4}
times. Thus, for any
ivk,isig
, the probability that
(ivk,isig)\not\in X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}
is at most
\exp\{-r_{OSS,\ell^{*}}(\lambda)\cdot\alpha_{ivk,isig}\}
. Since we have

1-\exp\{-r_{OSS,\ell^{*}}(\lambda)\cdot\frac{1}{512\cdot s_{OSS,\ell^{*}}(\lambda)\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}\}=1-\exp{-\lambda}

the claim follows. Now define
\mathsf{GOOD}
to be the event that all
(ivk,isig)\in\mathfrak{L}
that satisfies

\alpha_{ivk,isig}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}}\geq\frac{1}{512\cdot s_{OSS,\ell^{*}}(\lambda)\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

are included in
X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}
. Since this is true with probability
1-\exp\{-\lambda\}
for each
ivk,isig\in\mathfrak{L}
individually, and since
|\mathfrak{L}|
is polynomial, we have that
\Pr[\mathsf{GOOD}]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
.

Now we claim

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}\big|\mathsf{GOOD}]\leq\frac{1}{512\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

To see this, first by linearity of expectation we have

\displaystyle\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}\big|\mathsf{GOOD}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,isig:\\
(ivk,isig)\not\in X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}\\
(ivk,isig)\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}}\left[\alpha_{ivk,isig}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}}\big|\mathsf{GOOD}\right].

However, by definition of
\mathsf{GOOD}
, when
\mathsf{GOOD}
occurs, we have

ivk,isig\in\mathfrak{L}\setminus X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}\implies\alpha_{ivk,isig}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2}}\leq\frac{1}{512\cdot s_{OSS,\ell^{*}}(\lambda)\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

Since
|\mathfrak{L}|=s_{OSS,\ell^{*}}(\lambda)
, we obtain the desired upper bound on

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}\big|\mathsf{GOOD}].

Since
\Pr[\mathsf{GOOD}]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
, we get

\mathds{E}_{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}]\leq\frac{1}{256\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

∎

Now, observe that
Lemma

15
follows from these two lemmata. To see this, observe that
W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}}
is an upper bound on the total weight on inputs at which the two oracles might differ. Since we have
W^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}}=W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}},\mathfrak{L}}+W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*}-1,2},X^{\ell^{*}}_{0,i^{*}}\mathfrak{L}}
with probability
1
, the result follows by the linearity of expectation,
Theorem

6
and Jensen’s inequality.

11.2
Proof of
Lemma

16

Fix any
i^{*}\in\{1,\dots,t(\lambda)\}
. Thus, we will prove
\left|{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i^{*},1}}-{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i^{*},2}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda)}
. The proof will follow similarly to
Lemma

9
.

Observe that the difference between the two hybrids is that in
{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i^{*},1}}
we run
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell^{*},i^{*},1}
directly, whereas in
{\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i^{*},2}}
we instead run
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell^{*},i^{*},2}
which simulates
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell^{*},i^{*},1}
while using an estimated oracle for its first query to
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{2}
. We will use the incompressibility property of
H_{0},H_{1}
to show that this only makes a difference of
\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}\cdot u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda)}
in statistical distance.

Let
\gamma_{ivk,y_{0},y_{1},z}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1}}
denote the query amplitude of
\mathcal{C}_{\ell^{*},2,i^{*},1}(Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1})
on input
ivk,y_{0},y_{1},z
during its first query to
\mathcal{O}^{*}_{2}
. We also define the following.

\displaystyle\alpha_{ivk,z}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1}}=\sum_{y_{0},y_{1}:\mathcal{O}^{*}_{2}(ivk,y_{0},y_{1},z)\neq\bot}|\gamma_{ivk,y_{0},y_{1},z}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1}}|^{2}

Now let
(X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}})_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}
,
(X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{1,j^{\prime}})_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}
,
(X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{2,j^{\prime}})_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]-1},(D^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}})_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}
,
(D^{\ell^{\prime}}_{1,j^{\prime}})_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}
,
(D^{\ell^{\prime}}_{2,j^{\prime}})_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]-1}
be the estimated lists and databases in
Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1}
when it is parsed and fully unwrapped. With abuse of notation, sometimes we will refer to the projection of the sets
X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{\cdot,j^{\prime}}
onto their elements’ (which are tuples) subcomponents also as
X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{\cdot,j^{\prime}}
(e.g. we will write
X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{2,j^{\prime}}
to mean the set of pairs
(ivk,z)
obtained by projecting each tuple in the actual set
X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{2,j^{\prime}}
to its first and fourth elements) - however, the meaning will clear from context. Now we claim the following.

Claim 10
.

\Pr\left[\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)\bigcap\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{1,j^{\prime}}\right)=\emptyset\right]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(\lambda).

Proof.

Observe that if this intersection is not empty, then there is
ivk
such that
ivk\in X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}
and
ivk\in X^{\ell^{\prime\prime}}_{1,j^{\prime\prime}}
for some
\ell^{\prime},\ell^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime},j^{\prime\prime}
. However, by construction we know that this implies that we can obtain
isig_{0},isig_{1}
from
Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1}
such that
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Ver}^{*}}(ivk,0,isig_{0})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Ver}^{*}}(ivk,1,isig_{1})=1
. Since
Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1}
is obtained by making polynomially many queries to the oracles of
\mathsf{OSS}
, the claim follows by the one-shot security of
\mathsf{OSS}
.
∎

Now we define the following (some dependencies are implicit to keep the notation concise).

\displaystyle W_{0}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1},X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,z:\\
ivk\not\in\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)\\
(ivk,z)\not\in X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,z}

\displaystyle W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1},X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,z:\\
ivk\not\in\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{1,j^{\prime}}\right)\\
(ivk,z)\not\in X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,z}

\displaystyle W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1},X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,z:\\
ivk\in\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)\bigcap\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{1,j^{\prime}}\right)\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,z}

Now we make the following claims.

Claim 11
.

\mathds{E}[W_{2}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1},X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}}]\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda).

(2)

Proof.

This simply follows from the definition of
W_{2}
and
Claim

10
.
∎

Claim 12
.

For all sufficiently large
\lambda>0
.

\displaystyle\mathds{E}[W_{0}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1},X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}}]\leq\frac{1}{128\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathds{E}[W_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1},X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}}]\leq\frac{1}{128\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

Proof.

We will only prove the first one (using incompressibility of the random oracle
H_{0}
), and the second bound follows by the same argument (but this time relying on the incompressibility of
H_{1}
).

Now we will use the incompressibility property (
Theorem

15
) of the random oracle
H_{0}
with the predicate
P
defined as
P(ivk||z)=1
if
ivk\in S
, where
S=\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)
. Note that this set
S
is exactly the set of prefixes on which our adversary has
section queries
(otherwise it only makes normal queries and verification queries). In more detail, let
\mathcal{P}_{1}
be the adversary that simulates
\mathsf{Hyb}^{\prime}_{i^{*},1}
until the point where
\mathcal{C}_{1,\ell^{*},i^{*},2}
outputs its message, and it also outputs
S
as its predicate. Then
\mathcal{P}_{2}
simulates
\mathcal{C}_{2,\ell^{*},i^{*},1}
until right after its first query to
\mathcal{O}_{2}^{*}
. We invoke
Theorem

15
for this pair of adversaries, and let
\mathfrak{L}
denote the incompressibility list that is promised to exist, and we know
|\mathfrak{L}|=\ell^{*}\cdot t(\lambda)
since
\mathcal{P}_{1}
makes at most
\ell^{*}\cdot t(\lambda)
normal queries. We then split
W_{0}
into two summands
U_{0},U_{1}
:

\displaystyle U_{0}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1},X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,z:\\
ivk\not\in\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)\\
(ivk,z)\not\in X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}\\
(ivk,z)\not\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,z}

\displaystyle U_{1}^{{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1},X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,z:\\
ivk\not\in\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)\\
(ivk,z)\not\in X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}\\
(ivk,z)\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{ivk,z}

It is easy to see that
\mathds{E}[U_{0}]\leq\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
by the incompressibility of
H_{0}
, thus, we will focus on bounding
U_{1}
.

First we claim that, for all
{\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathsf{QKeyFireSign}}},Y^{\ell^{*},i^{*},1}
, with overwhelming probability we have that any
ivk,z
that satisfies

\alpha_{ivk,z}\geq\frac{1}{512\cdot(t(\lambda)\cdot\ell^{*})\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

is contained in
X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}
. To see this, observe that
X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}
is created by measuring the first query of
\mathcal{C}_{\ell^{*},2,i^{*},1}
to
\mathcal{O}_{2}
for
512\cdot\lambda\cdot(t(\lambda)\cdot\ell^{*})\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}
times. Thus, for any
ivk,z
such that
\alpha_{ivk,z}
satisfies the above condition, the probability that
(ivk,z)\not\in X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}
is at most
\exp{-\lambda}
. Now define
\mathsf{GOOD}
to be the event that all
(ivk,z)\in\mathfrak{L}
that satisfies

\alpha_{ivk,z}\geq\frac{1}{512\cdot(t(\lambda)\cdot\ell^{*})\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

are included in
X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}
. Since this is true with probability
1-\exp\{-\lambda\}
for each
ivk,z\in\mathfrak{L}
individually, and since
|\mathfrak{L}|
is polynomial, we have that
\Pr[\mathsf{GOOD}]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
.

Now we claim

\mathds{E}[U_{1}\big|\mathsf{GOOD}]\leq\frac{1}{512\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

To see this, first by linearity of expectation we have

\displaystyle\mathds{E}[U_{1}\big|\mathsf{GOOD}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}ivk,z:\\
ivk\not\in\left(\bigcup_{\ell^{\prime}\in[\ell^{*}],j^{\prime}\in[i^{*}]}X^{\ell^{\prime}}_{0,j^{\prime}}\right)\\
(ivk,z)\not\in X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}\\
(ivk,z)\in\mathfrak{L}\end{subarray}}\mathds{E}[\alpha_{ivk,z}|\mathsf{GOOD}]

However, by definition of
\mathsf{GOOD}
, when
\mathsf{GOOD}
occurs, we have

ivk,z\in\mathfrak{L}\setminus X^{\ell^{*}}_{2,i^{*}}\implies\alpha_{ivk,z}\leq\frac{1}{512\cdot(t(\lambda)\cdot\ell^{*})\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

Since
|\mathfrak{L}|=\ell^{*}\cdot t(\lambda)
, we obtain the desired upper bound on expectation conditioned on
\mathsf{GOOD}
. Since
\Pr[\mathsf{GOOD}]\geq 1-\mathsf{negl}(\lambda)
, we also get

\mathds{E}[U_{1}]\leq\frac{1}{256\cdot\lambda^{4}\cdot(u(\lambda)\cdot q^{*}(\lambda)\cdot t(\lambda))^{2}}

which completes the proof.
∎

Observe that
Lemma

16
follows from the above claims. To see this, observe that
W_{0}+W_{1}+W_{2}
is an upper bound on the total weight on inputs at which the two different oracles in the hybrids might differ. Thus the result follows by the linearity of expectation,
Theorem

6
and Jensen’s inequality.

Appendix

Appendix A
Alternative Equivalent Definitions for Quantum Protection Notions

We recall the definition of LOCC leakage-resilience for the case of signing and decryption. Note that this is a special case of the general definition (
Definition

8
), and we write the specialized version explicitly for ease of exposition.

Definition 25

(Signature Scheme with LOCC Leakage-Resilience
[
ÇGLZR24
,
ÇG24
]
)
.

Consider the following game between the challenger and an adversary
\mathcal{A}
.

\mathsf{LR-SIGN-GAME}_{\mathcal{A}}(1^{\lambda})

1.

The challenger samples
vk,\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{key}}\leftarrow\mathsf{SIG}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and submits
vk
to
\mathcal{A}
.

2.

The adversary obtains leakage on
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{key}}
adaptively over any number of rounds as in
Definition

8
.

3.

The challenger samples
m^{*}\leftarrow\mathcal{M}
and submits
m^{*}
to
\mathcal{A}
.

4.

\mathcal{A}
outputs a forged signature
sig
.

5.

The challenger outputs
1
if and only if
\mathsf{SIG}.\mathsf{Ver}(vk,m^{*},sig)
.

A digital signature scheme
\mathsf{SIG}
is said to satisfy
LOCC leakage-resilience
if for any QPT adversary
\mathcal{A}
,

\Pr[\mathsf{LR-SIGN-GAME}_{\mathcal{A}}(1^{\lambda})=1]\leq\frac{1}{|\mathcal{M}|}+\mathsf{negl}(\lambda).

Definition 26

(Public-Key Encryption with LOCC Leakage-Resilience
[
ÇGLZR24
,
ÇG24
]
)
.

Consider the following game between the challenger and an adversary
\mathcal{A}
.

\mathsf{LR-PKE-GAME}_{\mathcal{A}}(1^{\lambda})

1.

The challenger samples
pk,\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{key}}\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Setup}(1^{\lambda})
and submits
pk
to
\mathcal{A}
.

2.

The adversary obtains leakage on
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{key}}
adaptively over any number of rounds as in
Definition

8
.

3.

The adversary
\mathcal{A}
outputs two messages
m_{0},m_{1}
.

4.

The challenger samples
b\leftarrow\{0,1\}
and
ct\leftarrow\mathsf{PKE}.\mathsf{Enc}(pk,m_{b})
and submits
ct
to
\mathcal{A}
.

5.

\mathcal{A}
outputs a guess
b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}
.

6.

The challenger outputs
1
if and only if
b^{\prime}=b
.

A public-key encryption scheme
\mathsf{PKE}
is said to satisfy
LOCC leakage-resilience
if for any QPT adversary
\mathcal{A}
,

\Pr[\mathsf{LR-PKE-GAME}_{\mathcal{A}}(1^{\lambda})=1]\leq\frac{1}{2}+\mathsf{negl}(\lambda).

Appendix B
On
[
HV25
]

The initial version (v1) of our paper on arXiV contained a small gap in last hybrid of our quantum key-fire proof, which was communicated to us by the authors of
[
HV25
]
and at the same time they put their paper online. Within a day of receiving their communication, we fixed the small gap in our proof and let them know. Current version of our paper does not have this gap (and it has additional results, such as
LOCC leakage-resilience security
for key-fire and unbounded-message-length incompressibility for OSS) however, we include a discussion here for historical reference.

In
[
HV25
]
, the authors claimed the following (about the first version of our paper).

1.

They claimed that the oracles
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{0}
that we set to be the signing oracle (
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{OSS}.\mathsf{Sign}}
) in our incompressible one-shot signature (OSS) scheme construction are not sufficient for signing, and they claimed that thus our claim about existence of
1
-bit incompressible OSS was incorrect.

2.

They claimed that incompressible OSS for multi-bit messages does not follow directly from incompressible OSS for
1
-bit messages.

In the following sections we address both of these points in detail. However, a quick summary is given below:

•

The first point mentioned was a misunderstanding by the authors of
[
HV25
]
regarding the prior work of
[
SZ25
]
and our construction.
[
HV25
]
claimed that the oracles
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{0}
are not sufficient for our signing algorithm, then proceeded to change our signing algorithm (they did not ask us for a clarification) by giving additional access to
\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{-1}
, and finally, claimed that our argument is flawed since incompressibility cannot hold given access to oracles
\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{-1}
. However, this is incorrect: It is indeed possible to sign using only the oracles
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{0}
as shown by various works (
[
AGKZ20
,
Shm22
,
SZ25
]
).

•

The second point was correct and indeed pointed to a small gap in the first version of our key-fire proof: We had implicitly assumed incompressible OSS for multi-bit messages follows in a straightforward manner from incompressible OSS for 1-bit messages, thus we had only given a 1-bit incompressible OSS scheme explicitly.

However, first, our quantum fire security proof (as opposed to key-fire, which is a slightly stronger primitive) had already relied on only incompressible OSS for 1-bit messages, and thus this gap did not effect our original quantum fire security proof.
Second, for proving key-fire, we had relied on incompressible OSS for multi-bit messages only in the last hybrid of the proof. Thus, this was a localized gap and can be fixed it with a small change to make it rely on only 1-bit incompressible OSS. Overall, it did not affect the main ideas and techniques of our paper. We communicated this small fix to them within a day of receiving their email.

More details now follow.

B.1
Signing Oracle

In our incompressible OSS scheme, we have four oracles
\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{-1},\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{0}
. We set
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{D}_{0}
as the signing and signature verification oracles. We proved that in the presence of the signing and verification oracle (that is
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{0}
), our scheme satisfies an incompressibility property.

The authors of
[
HV25
]
claimed that
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{0}
are not sufficient for signing, and they claimed that we must include
\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{-1}
to the signing oracle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{Sign}}
. Then they point out that given
\mathcal{P}
,
\mathcal{P}^{-1}
, the incompressibility property is broken. However, indeed the oracles
\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}_{0}
are sufficient for signing and we do not need to include
\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{-1}
. In this version, we added a part that recalls the details of the signing algorithm (which is entirely from the previous work) to prevent any future confusion.

To explain in more detail, in the one-shot signature literature, there are two main/well-known signing algorithms, the so-called Grover-style signing algorithm (from
[
AGKZ20
]
), and the generic signing algorithm from
[
DS23
]
. The confusion from
[
HV25
]
might be due to the fact that we said that our signing algorithm is as in
[
SZ25
]
, however
[
SZ25
]
does not explicitly write out its signing algorithm but rather it is implicit that the signing algorithm of
[
SZ25
]
is the Grover-style signing algorithm. In the one-shot signature literature, it is well-known that the Grover style signing algorithm is used when the quantum signature token takes the specific form of a coset state (i.e., a uniform superposition over vectors from some coset), which is the case for
[
SZ25
]
. This algorithm (with small modifications) has been used in many OSS works such as
[
AGKZ20
]
and
[
Shm22
]
, and it is well understood that this is the signing algorithm for coset based one-shot signature schemes like
[
SZ25
]
. Indeed, another follow-up work to
[
SZ25
]
also mentions this Grover-style signing algorithm as the signing algorithm of the
[
SZ25
]
construction: See Section 2.3, Page 11 in
[
BGMS25
]
where it is also clear that the only oracle being used for signing is
\mathcal{D}
. Note that
[
BGMS25
]
was put on arXiv on October 10th, which is prior to the publication of
[
HV25
]
.

Perhaps another point of the confusion from
[
HV25
]
is because
[
SZ25
]
essentially constructs both a one-shot signature scheme and a stronger primitive called a non-collapsing collision-resistant hash (non-collapsing CRH). Specifically,
[
SZ25
]
cites a known result by
[
DS23
]
, which says that a non-collapsing CRH generically implies an OSS. However, the context is to claim a stronger primitive (non-collapsing CRH over OSS), and they do not not mean that the specific construction at hand needs to use this generic transformation to get an OSS. Indeed, in the specific case of the construction from
[
SZ25
]
, their construction is both an OSS directly and a non-collapsing CRH, and the well-known Grover-style signing is to be used for signing when the goal is to obtain OSS directly.

B.2
Multi-bit Incompressible OSS

We showed the existence of incompressible OSS for 1-bit messages. 1-bit OSS is sufficient for most of our proof, but in the first version of our paper, in the proof of our last hybrid in key-fire security, we had invoked incompressible OSS for multi-bit messages. We had implicitly assumed incompressible OSS for multi-bit messages follow in a straightforward manner from incompressible OSS for 1-bit messages, thus we had only given a 1-bit incompressible OSS scheme explicitly. However in a nice observation,
[
HV25
]
point out that parallel repetition of a 1-bit OSS scheme to obtain multi-bit OSS scheme, does not preserve incompressibility.

This was a localized issue, and did not affect the rest of our paper. Moreover, this affected only key-fire: Without any changes, our quantum fire security (as opposed to key-fire, which is a slightly stronger primitive) in the first version of our paper still holds using only 1-bit OSS. For key-fire security, by making a small change to our construction and to the last hybrid, we made the proof work using only 1-bit incompressible OSS. When the gap was first pointed out to us by the authors of
[
HV25
]
, we communicated this fix to them within a day of receiving their email.
